--- original brief ---
# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Independent relation-map review ??round 28

Reviewed **HEAD `23b07f55dcb8a0d9a3416c0f0f8d46ffc9ef0c41`**, on 2026-11-04 (Asia/Seoul). **CHANGES REQUIRED.** Three P1 identity mechanisms remain: affirmative focus wording is still negated by the label guard; common cyanine/rhodamine abbreviations evade the dye guard; and an existing ADC's blanket exemption discards an explicitly recognized extra modification. All three also occur with the previous query module, so these are inherited gaps rather than regressions introduced by the latest commit.

The exact eight strict M27 leaks are now **FIXED**. That result and **355 passing tests** do not close the broader guarantee. Fresh evidence confirms **11 cases where every recognizer misses**, plus **3 cases where joining is recognized but the ADC exemption suppresses refusal**. All 14 pass complete map and trial/sponsor QA evidence equality checks against their corresponding unmodified drug. The actual page callbacks generate the same incorrect centre SVG and trail. [Direct and HTTP assertions](attacks/assertions.json), [page assertions](contract/current_callback_assertions.json), [pytest output](checks/pytest.txt), [pytest execution](checks/pytest_summary.json).

## Method and controls

Repair notes were hypotheses, not verification. Earlier reproduction scripts were reused with recorded hashes and output/port adaptations; the history and contract tables below give **FIXED / NOT FIXED per finding**, including exact primary and supporting inputs. M2's incomplete work and completed M2b are covered; M27 uses `attacks/FINDINGS.md` because there is no completed report. User-recorded over-refusals and the two owner-curation data limitations are retained separately and do not drive the blocker verdict.

The attack comparison contains **48 current/HEAD^ pairs**. The root independently ran the **unmodified `python src/app.py` CLI** on owned port **9955**, making **200 HTTP requests for 50 inputs** across resolve, explore, and trial/sponsor ask endpoints. All returned HTTP 200; page bytes equal the worktree, the database hash is unchanged, and the port is closed. HTTP success is not semantic correctness. The two extra inputs independently confirm the historical fragmented qualifiers `b.i.o.similar bevacizumab` and `lip.osomal doxorubicin`. [HTTP capture](checks/independent_m28_http.json), [lifecycle](checks/independent_m28_lifecycle.json), [reuse manifest](checks/http_reuse_manifest.json).

All **50 actual page form callbacks** run without rendering errors and reproduce their response's centre or refusal state. This is the real page JavaScript in a **Node VM with controlled DOM/fetch**, not browser painting or browser-executed XSS. HEAD^ comparisons load only its `query.py` alongside current modules/data; they are not historical deployments. Diagnostic ablations replace helpers/globals in memory, restore them, and clear caches; they identify causes and are not proposed production patches. No application code was edited and no model was called. [Current source excerpts](checks/source_excerpts.md), [causal ablations](attacks/counterfactual.json).


## M28-B1 ??focus-word repair is absent from the label guard (P1)

Exact primary: **`trastuzumab not only radiolabelled but also purified`**. This affirms radiolabelling and adds purification. Both current and previous query modules return **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, the complete bare-trastuzumab map (**13 graph trials**) and the complete bare trial/sponsor QA evidence (**12 / 12 rows**). The original CLI independently returns the same complete map and evidence arrays. Every actual refusal stage misses: no BLOCKED resolver component, `_resolver_blocked={}`, `_qualified=False`, `_item_has_isotope=False`, `_item_joined=False`, every `_marked` call returns None, `_whole_name=None`, no public or QA Refused entity. [Stage trace](attacks/new_results.json), [strict direct/HTTP assertions](attacks/assertions.json), [original CLI capture](checks/independent_m28_http.json).

`_FOCUS` is declared at `src/query.py:243-244` and used by `_item_joined` at `267`; `_item_has_isotope` at `302-305` still starts negation after every `not`. `_negated_end` at `218-219` sees `only` ending in `ly` and extends that negation onto the following strong label `radiolabelled`. The successful conjugation-focused repair therefore leaves the same affirmative semantics broken in the independent label path. `_marked` at `585-587` receives False, and `Engine.explore` at `src/app.py:88-95` draws the ordinary hit.

Seven supporting complete affirmative inputs reproduce the same strict all-stage leak: `trastuzumab not merely radiolabelled but also purified`; `trastuzumab not solely radiolabelled but also purified`; `trastuzumab not only labelled with Alexa Fluor 488 but also purified`; `trastuzumab not merely labelled with Alexa Fluor 488 but also purified`; `trastuzumab not only tagged with biotin but also purified`; `trastuzumab not simply tagged with biotin but also purified`; `trastuzumab not only radioactive but also purified`.

Controls isolate semantics: `trastuzumab only radiolabelled` correctly refuses; true negatives `trastuzumab not radiolabelled` and `trastuzumab not labelled with Alexa Fluor 488` retain TRASTUZUMAB. All seven listed focus words with positive DM1 conjugation are covered by the combined prior/new cases and correctly refuse. Adding only `and flat[i] not in _FOCUS` to the label guard **in memory** makes all eight focus-label leaks refuse, preserves the genuine negatives and ordinary controls, and leaves the dye-code and ADC-exemption leaks unchanged. The helper and caches are restored. [Exact original/replacement and causal outputs](attacks/counterfactual.json), [diagnostic script](attacks/counterfactual.py).

Identity premise does not require inferring any particular radiopharmaceutical. The affirmative input describes a labelled version and must not silently become the unlabelled drug. For the fluorescent corroborations, the manufacturer's description explicitly explains covalent attachment of Alexa Fluor dye or biotin to IgG. [Thermo Fisher antibody labeling](https://www.thermofisher.com/us/en/home/life-science/antibodies/antibody-labeling.html).

## M28-B2 ??standard cyanine and rhodamine abbreviations evade the named-dye guard (P1)

Exact primary: **`trastuzumab linked to Cy3`**; equally decisive corroboration: **`trastuzumab linked to TAMRA`**. Both current and previous versions draw the complete bare TRASTUZUMAB map and **12 / 12** QA arrays. The same strict all-stage assertions as B1 pass, including `_item_joined=False`. **`trastuzumab linked to BODIPY`** is a further named-dye-family corroboration; the blocker does not depend on resolving a particular BODIPY derivative. [Stage outputs](attacks/new_results.json), [equality assertions](attacks/assertions.json).

At `src/query.py:240-242`, the code list recognizes `cy5`, `cy7`, `cy55` but omits `cy3` and `tamra`. New `_REAGENTS` entries at `248-251` recognize strings starting `cyanin` or `rhodamin`; ordinary abbreviations have neither prefix. The pieces/reagent predicate at `263-265` is therefore False, and the `linked` branch at `280-284` misses. The literal controls `trastuzumab linked to cyanine 3`, `trastuzumab linked to rhodamine`, `trastuzumab linked to Cy5.5`, and both Alexa cases refuse.

The identity premise is manufacturer-supported, not inferred from an arbitrary capitalized token: Cytiva calls Cy3 a fluorescent cyanine and provides an antibody-conjugation protocol. Thermo Fisher identifies TAMRA as a tetramethylrhodamine derivative used for protein conjugates. [Cytiva Cy3 specification](https://d3.cytivalifesciences.com/prod/IFU/28951573.pdf), [Thermo Fisher rhodamine reference](https://www.thermofisher.com/us/en/home/references/molecular-probes-the-handbook/fluorophores-and-their-amine-reactive-derivatives/long-wavelength-rhodamines-texas-red-dyes-and-qsy-quenchers.html). These sources establish dye identity and the modification interpretation; they do not establish a unique registered trastuzumab-dye product.

Adding only `cy3`, `tamra`, and `bodipy` to the existing code set in memory makes the three dye cases refuse, retains nine ordinary/negative/bare-ADC controls, and leaves the label-focus and ADC-exemption defects intact. This diagnoses vocabulary coverage; it is not a proposed complete dye dictionary. [Causal outputs](attacks/counterfactual.json).

## M28-B3 ??a recognized extra dye or carrier is ignored for an existing ADC (P1)

Exact primary: **`trastuzumab emtansine linked to Alexa Fluor 488`**. The current map and both complete QA evidence arrays equal **bare trastuzumab emtansine / CHEMBL1743082**, with **5 graph trials / 5 trial QA / 5 sponsor QA rows**. **`trastuzumab deruxtecan linked to Alexa Fluor 488`** similarly equals **CHEMBL4297844**, with **6 / 6 / 4** rows. **`trastuzumab emtansine conjugated to polyethylene glycol`** independently corroborates the same exemption. All three occur on HEAD^ too. [Full direct traces](attacks/new_results.json), [strict public-stage and HTTP equality](attacks/assertions.json).

These are deliberately separated from the 11 cases whose recognizers all return False: here `_item_joined=True`, including when called only on the text outside the matched ADC name. Nevertheless **every actual refusal stage still misses**: resolver unblocked, `_marked=None`, `_whole_name=None`, public linker and QA contain no Refused object, and the map draws the existing ADC. A True internal cue is not a refusal.

`_payload_name` at `src/query.py:234-236` returns True solely because the stored preferred name contains a later `emtansine` or `deruxtecan` word. The conjunction at `590-592` then suppresses the already-detected joining cue outside that matched name. The guard exempts the existing ADC from any additional linkage. This is broader than accepting an already stored ADC name: the new Alexa label is not part of either stored ADC name. The same manufacturer antibody-label chemistry above supports distinguishing a further dye-labelled antibody from the corresponding unlabelled ADC; no unique approved or measured clinical product is asserted.

In memory, replacing only `_payload_name` with `lambda name: False` makes these three extra-modification descriptions refuse while preserving the two bare ADC names in this measured cohort. It leaves B1 and B2 intact. This isolates the broad exemption; removing it globally is **not** offered as a completeness-tested production repair. An eventual repair must still preserve authentic stored ADC names and aliases. [Literal original helper, diagnostic replacement, independent controls and restoration](attacks/counterfactual.json).

Earlier script sources and hashes are recorded in [reuse manifest](attacks/reuse_manifest.json). No model was called; QA uses the local template generator.
- The known accepted over-refusals and owner-curation limitations do not explain these failures. Further-modified emtansine/deruxtecan is separate from the accepted `Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate` over-refusal.

### M24 joining-guard rescan cost: NOT FIXED as residual overhead

The guard at `src/query.py:590-591` constructs a cache key from the entire list item minus each matched span. With **`' '.join(f'isa x{i:04d}' for i in range(400))`** (3,999 characters, one list item), each removal yields a different tuple. The separate direct instrumented pass records **400 misses / 319,600 words / 1,278,800 aggregate characters** processed by `_item_joined`; its wrapped guard time is 0.37799 s out of 1.04880 s total. At 50 matches / 499 characters it records 50 misses / 4,950 words. These counts demonstrate growing rescan work, not merely host timing variation. [Instrumentation](contract/cost/joined_instrumentation.json).

For the same 3,999-character workload, the ABBA workers retain the same accepted entity in HEAD and HEAD^. HEAD / HEAD^ medians-of-worker-medians are **0.76389 / 0.67499 s**, and first-call cold medians are **1.14445 / 1.13367 s**. The original CLI HTTP check completes in **1.63036 s**, queues an EGFR map for **1.54216 s**, and a warm repeat completes in **1.52518 s**, all 200. [ABBA samples](contract/cost/joined_cost.json), [HTTP](contract/cost/isolated_joined_unique_cost_http.json). This is a **should-fix cost observation**, with no timeout or incorrect map proven by the performance tests. The same ABBA suite covers 499-character matched inputs, repeated conjugation descriptions, a conjugation after many aliases, and negated-adverb text; each completes under the existing character caps. The exact M27 repairs work in fresh direct and CLI HTTP evidence: forward/reverse SN-38, DM-1, Alexa 488/647 and complete `not merely/solely/exclusively ... but also ...` conjugation refuse. The genuine negative `not directly conjugated` and ordinary clinical-relation controls retain their drugs. Glued neighbouring pairs implement the stated spelling repair.

Three requirements remain incomplete:

The label guard at `query.py:302-305` negates the asserted label through `_negated_end:218-219`. Eight fresh variants leak.
2. Standard abbreviations for the stated dye families are absent: `trastuzumab linked to Cy3` and `... TAMRA` draw bare TRASTUZUMAB, while written-out cyanine/rhodamine refuse. Manufacturer references substantiate these names as dyes.
3. The blanket `_payload_name` exemption at `query.py:590-592` accepts an already recognized additional modification: `trastuzumab emtansine linked to Alexa Fluor 488` draws the unlabelled existing ADC. DERUXTECAN and PEG-modified EMTANSINE corroborate this independently.

All three are inherited residual defects, not new regressions in this commit. [FINDINGS.md](attacks/FINDINGS.md) supplies exact mechanism, complete map/QA equality, source links, controls and independent causal ablations. Fresh assertions confirm 11 all-recognizer misses plus 3 positive-cue/exemption leaks; no actual refusal stage rejects any of the 14. Specification verdict: **CHANGES REQUIRED**.


## Approval decision and limitations

**M28-B1, M28-B2 and M28-B3 each violate the distinct-product identity guarantee.** The exact M27 cases are fixed, while the new complete affirmative label, standard dye abbreviation, and extra-modification-on-ADC inputs still draw an unmodified constituent and its complete evidence. A successful graph join cannot repair incorrect product identity at the centre. M28-B3 independently adjudicates the same blanket exemption behind the retained M24/M25 extra-dye ADC diagnostics, using source-supported Alexa modification inputs and complete evidence equality.

Two **non-waived historical originator leaks also remain**: `b.i.o.similar bevacizumab` draws BEVACIZUMAB and `lip.osomal doxorubicin` draws DOXORUBICIN. They were independently confirmed by the root CLI HTTP capture and remain NOT FIXED in the history matrix; they are additional reasons to withhold approval, not new M28 findings or accepted exceptions.

No new live-edge membership, direction/relation wording, stored-value or fixed-page demo failure was reproduced; generated-markup safety checks also pass within the Node VM limitation above. Cost and documentation observations are reported separately as should-fix items. Historical **NOT FIXED** diagnostics remain explicitly unresolved; they are not silently waived or added to the user's exceptions. The accepted over-refusals and ChEMBL bevacizumab-code / AACT isotretinoin?밫RETINOIN curation limitations do not explain any of the three new blockers.

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m28/checks/finalize_integrity.py
import hashlib
import json
import re
import socket
import subprocess
from pathlib import Path

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROUND = OUT.parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
before = json.loads((OUT/'integrity_before.json').read_text(encoding='utf8'))
changed=[]
for p,digest in before['tracked'].items():
    file=ROOT/p
    if not file.is_file() or hashlib.sha256(file.read_bytes()).hexdigest()!=digest:
        changed.append(p)
head=subprocess.check_output(['git','rev-parse','HEAD'],cwd=ROOT,text=True).strip()
diff=subprocess.run(['git','diff','--exit-code','HEAD','--'],cwd=ROOT,capture_output=True,text=True)
status=subprocess.run(['git','status','--short'],cwd=ROOT,capture_output=True,text=True)
database=hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest()

report=(ROUND/'REPORT.md').read_text(encoding='utf8')
links=[]
for target in re.findall(r'\]\(([^\s)]+)\)',report):
    if re.match(r'^(?:[a-z]+:|/|#)',target): continue
    clean=target.split('#')[0]
    links.append({'target':target,'exists':(ROUND/clean).exists()})

lifecycles=[]
for file in ROUND.rglob('*lifecycle.json'):
    if 'contended' in file.parts: continue
    value=json.loads(file.read_text(encoding='utf8'))
    entry={'file':str(file.relative_to(ROUND)).replace('\\','/'),'data':value}
    if isinstance(value,dict) and isinstance(value.get('port'),int):
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            sock.settimeout(.25)
            entry['currently_closed']=sock.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1',value['port']))!=0
    lifecycles.append(entry)

result={'head_before':before['head'],'head_after':head,
        'tracked_file_count':len(before['tracked']),'changed_tracked_files':changed,
        'git_diff_exit_code':diff.returncode,'git_diff':diff.stdout,'git_status':status.stdout,
        'git_warnings':status.stderr,'database_sha256':database,
        'database_unchanged':database==before['database'],
        'report_links_checked':len(links),'missing_report_links':[x for x in links if not x['exists']],
        'lifecycles':lifecycles,
        'report_last_line':report.rstrip().splitlines()[-1]}
(OUT/'integrity_final.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
# The report links to this verification artifact itself. Recheck after writing it,
# so a first run in a clean round directory does not report its own output missing.
result['missing_report_links']=[x for x in links if not (ROUND/x['target'].split('#')[0]).exists()]
(OUT/'integrity_final.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps({k:v for k,v in result.items() if k not in ['lifecycles','git_warnings']},ensure_ascii=False,indent=2))
assert not changed and head==before['head'] and diff.returncode==0 and result['database_unchanged']
assert not result['missing_report_links']
assert all(x.get('currently_closed',True) for x in lifecycles)
assert result['report_last_line'] in ('VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED', 'VERDICT: APPROVE')

PORT = 9955
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
CASES = ['trastuzumab', 'trastuzumab linked to SN-38', 'SN-38 linked to trastuzumab', 'trastuzumab linked to SN38', 'trastuzumab linked to DM-1', 'trastuzumab linked to DM1', 'trastuzumab linked to Alexa Fluor 488', 'trastuzumab linked to Alexa Fluor 647', 'trastuzumab linked to fluorescein', 'trastuzumab linked to doxorubicin', 'trastuzumab not merely conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not solely conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not exclusively conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not only conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not directly conjugated to DM1', 'trastuzumab is not only linked to DM1 but also purified', 'irinotecan linked to polyethylene glycol', 'polyethylene glycol linked to irinotecan', 'osimertinib linked to longer PFS', 'osimertinib linked to improved 3-year survival', 'osimertinib linked to response in 2026', 'osimertinib linked to resistance in EGFR', 'trastuzumab not only labelled with Alexa Fluor 488 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not merely labelled with Alexa Fluor 488 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not only radiolabelled but also purified', 'trastuzumab not merely radiolabelled but also purified', 'trastuzumab not solely radiolabelled but also purified', 'trastuzumab not only tagged with biotin but also purified', 'trastuzumab not simply tagged with biotin but also purified', 'trastuzumab not only radioactive but also purified', 'trastuzumab only radiolabelled', 'trastuzumab not radiolabelled', 'trastuzumab not labelled with Alexa Fluor 488', 'trastuzumab not just conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not simply conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not purely conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab linked to Cy3', 'trastuzumab linked to Cy5.5', 'trastuzumab linked to cyanine 3', 'trastuzumab linked to rhodamine', 'trastuzumab linked to BODIPY', 'trastuzumab linked to TAMRA', 'trastuzumab linked to SN-38 through a linker', 'trastuzumab emtansine conjugated to polyethylene glycol', 'trastuzumab emtansine', 'trastuzumab emtansine linked to Alexa Fluor 488', 'trastuzumab deruxtecan linked to Alexa Fluor 488', 'trastuzumab deruxtecan', 'b.i.o.similar bevacizumab', 'lip.osomal doxorubicin']


def post(endpoint, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + "/api/" + endpoint,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    with response:
        return {"endpoint": endpoint, "input": text, "status": response.status,
                "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start,
                "response": json.loads(response.read())}

(OUT/"tmp").mkdir(exist_ok=True)
with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "owned port already occupied"
db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1", "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TEMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TMPDIR":str(OUT/"tmp")}
with (OUT / "independent_m28_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    results = []
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                page = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        for name in CASES:
            for endpoint in ("resolve", "explore"):
                results.append(post(endpoint, name))
            if not name.startswith("CHEMBL"):
                results.append(post("ask", f"Which trials evaluate {name}?"))
                results.append(post("ask", f"Who sponsors trials of {name}?"))
        (OUT / "independent_m28_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(results,
            ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        print(json.dumps([{"input": r["input"], "status": r["status"],
            "center": r["response"].get("center", {}).get("id"),
            "refused": r["response"].get("refused")}
            for r in results if r["endpoint"] == "explore"], indent=2))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        (OUT / "independent_m28_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "port_closed": closed,
            "database_unchanged": hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == before,
            "requests": len(results), "all_200": all(r["status"] == 200 for r in results),
            "page_matches_worktree": page == (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_bytes()
        }, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

205: def _negated_end(flat: list, i: int) -> int:
206:     """The end of the label a negation just before flat[i] governs: the shortest glued run
207:     that is a known label ("non-labelled", "non-radio-labelled"), a bare "radio" with the
208:     word it prefixes ("non-radio-astatinated"), and a label after its adverb ("not
209:     radioactively labelled"); never a separate later label ("non-labelled radiolabelled")."""
210:     end = i + 1
211:     for j in range(i + 1, min(len(flat), i + 3) + 1):
212:         if _strong_label("".join(flat[i:j])):
213:             end = j
214:             break
215:     else:
216:         if flat[i] == "radio" and i + 1 < len(flat):
217:             end = i + 2
218:     if flat[end - 1].endswith("ly") and end < len(flat) and _strong_label(flat[end]):
219:         end += 1
220:     return end

597:         # "trastuzumab Ga68"), refuses an unlabelled drug. A drug whose own name carries a
598:         # label (LUTETIUM LU 177 DOTATATE, FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE F 18) is the labelled product.
599:         if (_item_has_isotope(tuple(tokens[k] for k in same))
600:                 and not _labelled(self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"])):
601:             return name
602:         # a joining word inside the matched name is the conjugate's own name
603:         # ("cbr96 - doxorubicin immunoconjugate"), so only the rest of the item counts
604:         # a drug whose own name is the conjugate (TRASTUZUMAB EMTANSINE) keeps "antibody-drug
605:         # conjugate", but not a further dye or carrier named beside it ("... linked to Alexa
606:         # Fluor 488", "... conjugated to polyethylene glycol")
607:         rest = tuple(tokens[k] for k in same if not a <= k < b)
608:         if _item_joined(rest) and (not _payload_name(self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"])
609:                                    or _item_reagent(rest)):
610:             return name
611:         # A one-letter element is left to the case-marked word check above: lower-case
612:         # chemistry ("13-cis retinoic acid") is not carbon-13.
613:         # The drug's name may sit anywhere in the rest ("89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT"). A
614:         # one-letter element counts where it is written upper-case in the name
615:         # ("3HVINBLASTINE", "A33124I"); lower-case chemistry ("13-cis") does not.
616:         names = [k for k in self._keys_of(did) if len(k) >= 5]
617:         orig = "".join(re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(" ".join(tokens[a:b]))))
618:         if len(orig) != len(key):
619:             orig = key.upper()                   # cannot align: treat as marked
620:         for i in range(2, min(6, len(key))):
621:             for lab, tail, at in ((key[:i], key[i:], i - 1), (key[-i:], key[:-i], len(key) - 1)):
622:                 if not tail or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
623:                     continue
624:                 elem = re.sub(r"^\d+m?", "", lab)
625:                 if len(elem) == 1 and not orig[at].isupper():
626:                     continue
627:                 if any(k in tail for k in names):
628:                     return name
629:                 # a label closing a stored name of this drug ("A33124I"): the name itself
630:                 # is the labelled product. Only a mass used in medicine counts here, so a
631:                 # development code's salt letter ("NSC-118218H", "DX-8951F") is not a label
632:                 if (lab == key[-i:] and len(tail) >= 2 and key in self._keys_of(did)
633:                         and _medical_isotope(lab)):
634:                     return name
635: 
636:         return None
637: 
638:     def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict, found: list):
639:         """The whole input is exactly one stored drug name, letter for letter ("CP-358,774",
640:         "(E/Z)-AFATINIB"): read it whole rather than as the list its punctuation suggests.
641: 
642:         Only after every refusal has had its say: nothing the resolver or the list
643:         linker refused, and no product or tracer pattern in the name."""
644:         if blocked or any(e["type"] == "Refused" for e in found):
645:             return None
646:         name = text.strip(" ?!.")
647:         if T.is_distinct_product(name) or T.is_radiotracer(name):
648:             return None             # the list linker above has already applied the rest
649:         key = T.canonical_key(name)
650:         exact = [h for h in (self.dic["index"].get(key) or [])
651:                  if _fold(h["alias"]).strip(" ?!.").lower() == name.lower()]
652:         if not exact or len({h["drug_id"] for h in exact}) != 1:
653:             return None
654:         did = exact[0]["drug_id"]
655:         return {"type": "Drug", "id": did, "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
656:                 "matched_text": name, "matched_alias": exact[0]["alias"]}
657: 
658:     def _link(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
659:         found: list[dict] = []
660:         seen: set = set()
661:         tokens: list[str] = []
662:         clause: list[int] = []          # which list piece each token is in
663:         inner: list[bool] = []          # token inside parentheses
664:         ci, depth = 0, 0
665:         for piece in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question):

FILE src/app.py
1: """A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.
2: 
3:     python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765
4: 
5: A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
6: click to move on),
7: then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
8: how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
9: evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
10: claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
11: see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
12: the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
13: OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
14: pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
15: """
16: from __future__ import annotations
17: 
18: import json
19: import sqlite3
20: import sys
21: import threading
22: from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
23: from pathlib import Path
24: 
25: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
26: import config as C
27: import normalize as N
28: import textrules as T
29: from query import OncoKG
30: 
31: PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
32: PORT = 8765
33: 
34: 
35: class Engine:
36:     def __init__(self):
37:         self.kg = OncoKG()
38:         # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
39:         # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
40:         self.kg.conn.close()
41:         self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
42:         self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
43:         self.lock = threading.Lock()
44:         self.dic = self.kg.dic
45:         self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
46:         target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
47:         self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)
48: 
49:     def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
50:         out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
51:         for c in out["components"]:
52:             d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
53:             if d:
54:                 c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
55:                              ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
56:                 c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
57:                                            for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
58:                 c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
59:                     "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
60:                     (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
61:                 par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
62:                 if par:
63:                     c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
64:                         "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
65:                         (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
66:             if c.get("candidates"):
67:                 c["candidate_names"] = [
68:                     (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
69:         return out
70: 
71:     def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
72:         """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.
73: 
74:         `text` is a node id or a name the question linker reads as one entity. A name
75:         the linker refuses (a biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is reported, never
76:         mapped to the originator. Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the
77:         page states: trials by start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph,
78:         sponsors by trials they lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship
79:         keeps its role (lead sponsor or collaborator)."""
80:         conn = self.kg.conn
81:         also: list = []
82:         matched = None
83:         row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
84:                            (text,)).fetchone()
85:         if row:
86:             nid = row["id"]
87:         else:
88:             ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
89:             refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
90:             hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
91:             if not hits:
92:                 if refused:
93:                     return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
94:                 return {"not_found": text}
95:             nid, matched = hits[0]["id"], hits[0].get("matched_alias") or hits[0].get("matched_text")
96:             also = [{"id": e["id"], "type": e["type"], "label": e["label"]} for e in hits[1:]]
97:             for oid in hits[0].get("also_matching_ids") or []:   # one alias, several ids
98:                 lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
99:                                    (oid,)).fetchone()
100:                 if lab and oid != nid:
101:                     also.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
102:             # the same words may also name an organisation or a target the linker
103:             # passed over because a drug alias came first ("Roche" is a folic acid alias)
104:             key = T.canonical_key(text)
105:             for cid in self.kg.companies.get(key, []):
106:                 if cid != nid and all(x.get("id") != cid for x in also):
107:                     lab = conn.execute("SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
108:                                        (cid,)).fetchone()
109:                     if lab:
110:                         also.append({"id": cid, "type": "Company", "label": lab["label"]})
111:             t = self.kg.targets.get(text.strip().lower())
112:             if t and t["target_id"] != nid:
113:                 also.append({"id": t["target_id"], "type": "Target", "label": t["symbol"]})
114:             also += [{"refused": e["label"]} for e in refused]
115:         node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
116:                             "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
117:         if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
118:             return {"not_found": text}
119:         rows = conn.execute(
120:             "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "
121:             "n.props AS nprops FROM edge e JOIN node n "
122:             "ON n.id = CASE WHEN e.src=? THEN e.dst ELSE e.src END "
123:             "WHERE (e.src=? OR e.dst=?) AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND n.retired_at IS NULL",
124:             (nid, nid, nid)).fetchall()
125:         drug_ids = list({r["id"] for r in rows if r["type"] == "Drug"} | {nid})
126:         trials = {}
127:         for i in range(0, len(drug_ids), 500):
128:             chunk = drug_ids[i:i + 500]
129:             trials.update(conn.execute(               # live trials only
130:                 "SELECT e.dst, COUNT(*) FROM edge e JOIN node t ON t.id = e.src "
131:                 "WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND t.retired_at IS NULL "
132:                 f"AND e.dst IN ({','.join('?' * len(chunk))}) GROUP BY e.dst", chunk).fetchall())
133:         groups: dict = {}
134:         for r in rows:
135:             ep, np_ = _obj(r["eprops"]), _obj(r["nprops"])
136:             item = {"id": r["id"], "type": r["type"], "label": r["label"], "edge_id": r["eid"]}
137:             role = ep.get("role") if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS" else None
138:             if r["type"] == "Drug":
139:                 item["trials"] = trials.get(r["id"], 0)
140:                 item["max_phase"] = np_.get("max_phase_overall")
141:             if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF":
142:                 n = ep.get("evidence_trials")
143:                 # a count the edge does not record stays unknown; 0 is only for sorting
144:                 item["evidence_trials"] = n if _number(n) else None
145:                 item["weight"] = n if _number(n) else 0

The successful conjugation-focused repair therefore leaves the same affirmative semantics broken in the independent label path. `_marked` at `585-587` receives False, and `Engine.explore` at `src/app.py:88-95` draws the ordinary hit.

This is broader than accepting an already stored ADC name: the new Alexa label is not part of either stored ADC name. ---
name: computer-use
description: Control Windows apps from ChatGPT
---

# Computer Use

Use this skill to automate the UI of Microsoft Windows apps. It uses SendInput, UI Automation, and Windows.Graphics.Capture screenshots that work even when windows are occluded.

If this plugin is available, read this entire `SKILL.md` once before Windows automation work, before saying Computer Use is unavailable, and before falling back to other Windows automation.

Start with the directions in the Initialize section below. Read these bundled Markdown files relative to this `SKILL.md` when you need information about the specific topic they cover:

- `../../docs/guidance.md`: core runtime behavior, target-window workflow, screenshot handling, and recovery guidance. You MUST read this before controlling Windows apps.
- `../../docs/api.md`: full `sky` API reference. Read this when you need method signatures or object shapes.
- `../../docs/confirmations.md`: you MUST read this before deciding whether a Windows UI action needs confirmation

## Initialize

The bundled `cua_node` `@oai/sky` package is the core entry point for Computer Use. Import it directly by package name from the JavaScript session. Do not spawn `codex-computer-use.exe`, search for the helper executable, or build a custom helper protocol client.

Run this once per fresh `node_repl` JavaScript session:

```js
if (!globalThis.sky) {
  const { sky } = await import("@oai/sky");
  globalThis.sky = sky;
}
```

+"""Independently compare complete maps and QA evidence, not just centre labels."""
+import json
+from pathlib import Path
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+rows = json.loads((OUT / 'independent_new_http.json').read_text(encoding='utf8'))
+index = {(r['endpoint'], r['input']): r for r in rows}
+pairs = {
+    'trastuzumab linked to ATTO 647N': 'trastuzumab',
+    'trastuzumab linked to Cy3B': 'trastuzumab',
+    'trastuzumab linked to DY-647P1': 'trastuzumab',
+    'trastuzumab linked to CF488A': 'trastuzumab',
+    'trastuzumab linked to Pacific Blue': 'trastuzumab',
+    'trastuzumab emtansine conjugated to ATTO 647N': 'trastuzumab emtansine',
+    'trastuzumab emtansine conjugated to streptavidin': 'trastuzumab emtansine',
+    'trastuzumab emtansine linked to ATTO 647N': 'trastuzumab emtansine',
+    'trastuzumab deruxtecan conjugated to Cy3B': 'trastuzumab deruxtecan',
+    'b.i.o.similar bevacizumab': 'bevacizumab',
+    'lip.osomal doxorubicin': 'doxorubicin',
+}
+checks = []
+for text, parent in pairs.items():
+    result = index['explore', text]['response']
+    baseline = index['explore', parent]['response']
+    resolver = index['resolve', text]['response']
+    check = {'input': text, 'parent': parent,
+             'center': result.get('center', {}).get('id'),
+             'map_trials': result.get('center', {}).get('trials'),
+             'complete_map_equal': result == baseline,
+             'no_resolver_refusal': not any(c.get('tier') == 'BLOCKED' for c in resolver['components']),
+             'no_map_refusal': not result.get('refused')}
+    for label, template in [('trial', 'Which trials evaluate {}?'), ('sponsor', 'Who sponsors trials of {}?')]:
+        qa = index['ask', template.format(text)]['response']
+        control = index['ask', template.format(parent)]['response']
+        check[label + '_qa_evidence_equal'] = qa['evidence'] == control['evidence']
+        check[label + '_qa_count'] = len(qa['evidence'])
+        check[label + '_qa_no_refusal'] = not any(e['type'] == 'Refused' for e in qa['entities'])
+        check[label + '_qa_no_model'] = qa['generated_by'] == 'deterministic template (no language model)'
+    checks.append(check)
+    assert all(value for key, value in check.items() if key.endswith(('_equal', '_refusal', '_model'))), check
+
+prior = json.loads((OUT / 'independent_m29_http.json').read_text(encoding='utf8'))
+prior_index = {(r['endpoint'], r['input']): r for r in prior}
+repairs = [
+    'trastuzumab not only labelled with Alexa Fluor 488 but also purified',
+    'trastuzumab not merely labelled with Alexa Fluor 488 but also purified',
+    'trastuzumab not only radiolabelled but also purified',
+    'trastuzumab not merely radiolabelled but also purified',
+    'trastuzumab not solely radiolabelled but also purified',
+    'trastuzumab not only tagged with biotin but also purified',
+    'trastuzumab not simply tagged with biotin but also purified',
+    'trastuzumab not only radioactive but also purified',
+    'trastuzumab linked to Cy3', 'trastuzumab linked to TAMRA',
+    'trastuzumab linked to BODIPY',
+    'trastuzumab emtansine conjugated to polyethylene glycol',
+    'trastuzumab emtansine linked to Alexa Fluor 488',
+    'trastuzumab deruxtecan linked to Alexa Fluor 488',
+]
+repair_checks = []
+for text in repairs:
+    r = prior_index['explore', text]['response']
+    c = {'input': text, 'refused': bool(r.get('refused')), 'no_center': 'center' not in r}
+    for label, template in [('trial', 'Which trials evaluate {}?'), ('sponsor', 'Who sponsors trials of {}?')]:
+        qa = prior_index['ask', template.format(text)]['response']
+        c[label + '_refused'] = any(e['type'] == 'Refused' for e in qa['entities'])
+        c[label + '_no_evidence'] = not qa['evidence']
+    assert all(v for k, v in c.items() if k != 'input'), c
+    repair_checks.append(c)
+result = {'new_http_requests': len(rows), 'old_http_requests': len(prior),
+          'all_http_200': all(r['status'] == 200 for r in rows + prior),
+          'identity_checks': checks, 'm28_repairs': repair_checks,
+          'all_assertions_passed': True}
+assert result['all_http_200']
+(OUT / 'http_assertions.json').write_text(json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
+print(json.dumps({'identity_equalities': len(checks), 'm28_repairs': len(repair_checks),
+                  'http_requests': len(rows) + len(prior), 'passed': True}))

from pathlib import Path
import re,json
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent;ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
rows=[]
for n in range(1,28):
 p=ROOT/f'audit_evidence_claims/m{n}/REPORT.md'
 if n==2 and not p.exists():p=ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m2b/REPORT.md'
 if n==27:p=ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m27/attacks/FINDINGS.md'
 text=p.read_text(encoding='utf8')
 ids=sorted(set(re.findall(r'\bM\d+[-–][BSD]\d+\b',text)))
 rows.append({'round':n,'source':p.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),'finding_ids':ids})
report=OUT.parent/'REPORT.md'
result={'prior_reports':rows,'note':__doc__}
if report.exists():
 current=report.read_text(encoding='utf8'); ids=sorted({x for row in rows for x in row['finding_ids']})
 result.update(unique_prior_finding_ids=ids,missing_ids=[x for x in ids if x not in current])

from pathlib import Path
import json,os,subprocess,sys,time
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent;ROOT=Path.cwd()
env=dict(os.environ,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1',PYTHONUTF8='1',PYTHONIOENCODING='utf-8',TEMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TMPDIR=str(OUT/'tmp'))
rows=[]
for name in ['run27.py','run_spec27.py','replay/m27_exact/new_probe.py','root_checks/independent_m27_http.py','replay/compare_replays.py']:
    start=time.perf_counter();print('START',name,flush=True)
    with (OUT/(name.replace('/','_')+'.m28.stdout.txt')).open('wb') as log:
        p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-B',str(OUT/name)],cwd=ROOT,env=env,stdout=log,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,timeout=7200)
    row={'script':name,'exit_code':p.returncode,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-start};rows.append(row)
    (OUT/'execution28.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf8');print(json.dumps(row),flush=True)
    if p.returncode:raise SystemExit(p.returncode)

﻿# Independent replay coverage review

Scope: read-only review of m28/history preparation/runner code against the completed m26 historical inventory and m27/attacks/FINDINGS.md. No other lane's files were edited and no heavy probes were run.

- `prepare28.py` copies **143 distinct Python script paths**, not earlier result JSON. This is preparation coverage, not an execution count. Its m27/history output literals remap to m28/history; 114xx owned ports remap to 116xx. The old-round literals inspected in `adaptation_literal_check.json` are source-case extraction or comparison reads. No escaping output path was found in the reviewed preparation/runner and literal scan.
- The nested runner chain includes m1 partial/source-case extraction, completed m2b and intervening historical probes; m26's 39 current/previous pairs, recall pairs and independent spec probe; and the authoritative m27 22-case probe plus 88-request original CLI capture. `run_spec27.py` regenerates the missing-in-chain m26 spec output under the current replay directory. The absent original m22 scan generator is explicitly reconstructed by replaying all 58 saved exact inputs, with provenance.
- Current results are compared to the completed **m26** baseline intentionally. `compare_replays.py` fails missing-evidence coverage in `summarize28.py`, and preserves positive node IDs, refusal existence, map/evidence values while ignoring timing, wording and historical comparison objects. This is a regression comparator, not a fresh correctness oracle.
- The summary initially retains old dispositions, then applies specific newly verified repairs and adds m26/m27 rows. Therefore **every changed comparison file must be inspected before finalizing retained statuses**; the summary alone does not automatically rederive every old disposition. The history agent acknowledged this requirement and will inspect the final differences.
- The intended final ledger separates prepared copies from direct subprocess records. Some real children are invoked inside wrappers without a dedicated execution-manifest row (for example `root_checks/run_http.py` invokes `http_audit.py` and `regression_http.py`, saving logs and asserting successful exits). Such a script is **not directly recorded**, not necessarily unexecuted. Superseded wrappers and unused helper copies require separate explanations, not inflated completion counts.

At this review snapshot, the historical runner was still active and `execution28.json`, final `historical_table.md`, `checks.json` and `copy_execution_coverage.json` were not yet available. Consequently this document confirms inspected preparation/coverage design only; it does not certify completed execution. Final status/count checks must use those fresh artifacts once produced. No concrete missing finding or stale-result reuse was found in the inspected paths.


## Completed execution cross-check

The earlier pending-execution qualification is now closed. Independent reads of the completed raw disposition JSON and Markdown table give **182 unique finding rows: 150 FIXED / 32 NOT FIXED**. This supersedes the intermediate 181/149/32 count: final comparison review explicitly added the previously unlisted M25 `trastuzumab linked with mertansine` diagnostic as FIXED. Every row links to an existing fresh evidence file; no duplicate round/finding pairs were found.

The execution manifest independently totals **141 successful recorded subprocess invocations**, with no nonzero or missing exit status. The 143 copied paths comprise 136 paths with direct invocation records and seven separately explained paths: two children proved by their asserting wrapper, four superseded/duplicate/delegated wrappers or helpers, and one obsolete diagnostic ablation. The latter's original production inputs were rerun. Prepared-copy count is therefore not inflated into an execution count.

The 49-file baseline comparison has no missing evidence and exactly two changed files. Its four changed cases are reverse PEG attachment, linked-with-mertansine, uppercase RESISTANCE, and EGFR-resistance wording. Each is explicitly reviewed in `history/comparison_review.json`; unexpected differences = 0. The added mertansine row and clarified ordinary-relation row resolve the earlier caution about mechanically retained statuses. No additional naming-coverage hole or stale-output reuse was found within this reviewed ledger. DB/UI/server/cost coverage remains the separate contract/root lanes' responsibility.
